## Foreign support and funding

Main article: [Foreign involvement in the Yemeni civil war (2014–present)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_involvement_in_the_Yemeni_civil_war_\(2014%E2%80%93present\))

See also: [United States support for Saudi Arabian–led operations in Yemen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_support_for_Saudi_Arabian%E2%80%93led_operations_in_Yemen)



Saudi Arabia's UK-supplied [Eurofighter Typhoons](167667.md) are playing a central role in Saudi-led bombing campaign in Yemen.

[^cite_note-424]



The U.S. Navy has actively participated in the [Saudi-led naval blockade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blockade_of_Yemen),

[^cite_note-425]

&#x20;which humanitarian organizations argue has been the main contributing factor to the [outbreak of famine in Yemen](51686838.md).

[^cite_note-426]

In March 2015, President [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama) declared that he had authorized U.S. forces to provide logistical and intelligence support to the Saudis in their military intervention in Yemen.[^cite_note-427] Then-Deputy Secretary of State [Antony Blinken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antony_Blinken) said, "as part of that effort, we have expedited weapons deliveries, we have increased our intelligence sharing, and we have established a joint coordination planning cell in the Saudi operation centre."[^cite_note-428]

[NATO](21133.md) powers such as the United Kingdom and the United States support the Saudi Arabian–led intervention in Yemen primarily through arms sales and technical assistance.[^cite_note-bbc-com_2015-09-11_IYF-429] France has also sold weapons to Saudi Arabia.[^cite_note-HRW_2015-11-18_RBS-430] MSF emergency coordinator Karline Kleijer called the US, France and the UK part of the Saudi-led coalition, which imposed the weapons embargo and blocked all ships from entering Yemen with supplies.[^cite_note-theguardian-com_2015-11-09_CHG-431] Rights groups have criticized the countries for supplying arms, and accuse the coalition of using [cluster munitions](79745.md), which are banned in most countries.[^cite_note-UK-RTS_2015-12-22_UBS-432] Oxfam pointed out that Germany, Iran, and Russia have also reportedly sold arms to the conflicting forces.[^cite_note-oxfam-org-uk_2015-09-11_UAS-433] Tariq Riebl, head of programmes in Yemen for Oxfam, said, "it's difficult to argue that a weapon sold to Saudi Arabia would not in some way be used in Yemen," or "if it's not used in Yemen it enables the country to use other weapons in Yemen."[^cite_note-bbc-com_2015-09-11_IYF-429] Amnesty International urged the U.S. and the UK to stop supplying arms to Saudi Arabia and to the Saudi-led coalition.[^cite_note-independent-co-uk_2015-10-08_AIU-434] On August 3, 2019, a United Nations report said the US, UK and France may be complicit in committing war crimes in Yemen by selling weapons and providing support to the Saudi-led coalition which it accused of using starvation of civilians as a tactic of warfare.[^cite_note-435][^cite_note-436]

In 2016, the United States government sold \$1.3 billion of arms to Saudi Arabia despite concerns from officials that it could be complicit in war crimes through its support for the Saudi Arabian-led coalition in Yemen. U.S. government lawyers were unable to conclude whether the U.S. support for the coalition made them a co-belligerents under international law.[^cite_note-437]

Arms sales by the United Kingdom to [Saudi Arabia](349303.md) and the [United Arab Emirates](69328.md) in 2019 reportedly soared by £1 billion, a 300% increase from 2018. [Campaign Against Arms Trade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Campaign_Against_Arms_Trade) spokesperson Andrew Smith condemned the increase and claimed that the UK arms industry "is dominated by human rights abusers, despots and dictatorships."[^cite_note-438] According to official figures released by the [Department for International Trade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_for_International_Trade) (DIT), British firms, most notably [BAE Systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BAE_Systems), exported £11 billion worth of arms in 2019, becoming the second-largest arms exporter after the United States.[^cite_note-439] Although a June 2019 court ruling halted the sale of UK arms to the Saudis which could be used in Yemen, such sales resumed in June following a review by the British government.[^cite_note-440]

In January 2020, the U.S. [State Department](31975.md) told lawmakers that it was planning to permit [Raytheon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raytheon) to sell precision-guided missiles worth \$478 million to Saudi Arabia and expand its manufacturing inside the country, despite the kingdom's [human rights](13831.md) record and objections by both [Democratic](5043544.md) and [Republican](32070.md) lawmakers.[^cite_note-441] On February 4, 2021, the new President [Joe Biden](145422.md) announced an end to U.S. support for Saudi-led operations in Yemen.[^cite_note-442] However, U.S. arms sales have continued.[^cite_note-443][^cite_note-\:2-444]

In early June 2020, the [French government](3398091.md) published a report on the arms exports of 2019, where the sale of €1.4 billion arms was made to [Saudi Arabia](349303.md). [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) urged the French authorities to halt any arms sale to Saudi, considering that the country was accused of possible war crimes and human rights abuses in [Yemen](350939.md).[^cite_note-445] In July 2020, Amnesty International revealed that France had promoted a private military center to train Saudi troops and backed it both financially and politically. According to the report, France intended to train the Saudi soldiers in the operations of the latest versions of weapons that had already been used in the Yemeni conflict. The training center has been set up at the town of [Commercy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commercy) in [Meuse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meuse_\(department\)) with funds extracted from the French taxpayer's money, violating international treaties, as per Lebel.[^cite_note-446][^cite_note-447]

In September 2020, a [United Nations](31769.md) panel listed Canada among the countries who contributed to fueling the war in Yemen. Following that, 39 human rights organizations, arms-control groups and labor unions, including the [Public Service Alliance of Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_Service_Alliance_of_Canada), sent a joint letter to Canadian Prime Minister [Justin Trudeau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justin_Trudeau) urging him to end arms exports to [Saudi Arabia](349303.md).[^cite_note-448]

The Saudi-led coalition used a precision-guided munition developed in the United States in an air hit on a detention facility in Saada in northwestern Yemen that killed at least 80 people and injured over 200, according to Doctors Without Borders. The laser-guided bomb used in the raid was made by Raytheon, a U.S. defence company.[^cite_note-449]

On 2 February 2022, following a spate of missile assaults by Yemeni rebels, the U.S announced it would deploy a guided-missile destroyer and cutting-edge fighter jets to help defend the United Arab Emirates.[^cite_note-450]

On 6 February 2022, the commander of [U.S. Central Command](338482.md) arrived in the UAE to expand on previous Pentagon announcements to assist the country in bolstering its defenses following attacks in Yemen by Iranian-backed rebels.[^cite_note-451]

In a June 2022 joint analysis, *The Washington Post* in association with the Security Force Monitor, a research organization at [Columbia Law School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbia_Law_School)'s Human Rights Institute, reported that the United States supported the majority of the Saudi-led coalition's air force squadrons. A large portion of the coalition airstrikes were carried out by aircraft developed, maintained and sold by U.S. companies and were flown by pilots trained by the U.S. military. Out of the 39 air force units of the coalition that could have conducted airstrikes, 38 likely benefited from U.S.-approved contracts, and in the seven years since the beginning of the bombing campaign, the U.S. approved 213 out of the 902 total contracts that potentially served the coalition's air force units.[^cite_note-452][^cite_note-453]

On 7 June 2022, an internal report from the U.S. [Government Accountability Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_Accountability_Office) (GAO) was released which concluded that the State Department and the [Defense Department](7279897.md) had failed to properly assess war crimes allegations against the Saudi-led coalition and had not adequately tracked civilian deaths caused by U.S.-made weapons. The report concluded that there were serious gaps in U.S. government oversight on how arms sold to Saudi Arabia and the UAE were used.[^cite_note-454]

[Yemeni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yemeni) citizen Ayman Mohammed Saleh Al-Sanabani filed a lawsuit against Saudi Arabia and the UAE, the U.S. State and Defense departments, [Raytheon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raytheon), [Lockheed Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockheed_Martin) and [General Dynamics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Dynamics), accusing them of human rights abuses in Yemen. In an opinion piece published in *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)* in March 2023, he said his family, including his bride, were massacred on his wedding day in a missile attack by Saudi Arabia and the UAE in October 2015. Ayman Mohamed also accused the U.S. of complicity in the tragedy and war crimes in Yemen. He further accused the U.S. and its defense contractors of complicity in the humanitarian crisis in the country. He also called on President Biden to fulfill his campaign promise and stop the arms sales to Saudi Arabia.[^cite_note-455]

## Operation costs

In December 2015, David Ottaway, a senior scholar at the [Wilson Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woodrow_Wilson_International_Center_for_Scholars) in Washington, estimated that the Saudi-led military coalition was spending \$200 million a day on military operations in Yemen. His sources speculated that the Saudis were supplying most of the funding.[^cite_note-bloomberg-com_2015-12-21_STO-456]

On 20 October 2020, the Swiss [State Secretariat for Economic Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Secretariat_for_Economic_Affairs_\(Switzerland\)) published a report stating that Swiss companies exported war material to the value of almost 690 million francs. According to this report, Saudi Arabia bought war material from Switzerland for 3.8 million francs.[^cite_note-457]

## Responses

Further information: [International reactions to the Saudi Arabian-led intervention in Yemen (2015–present)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_reactions_to_the_Saudi_Arabian-led_intervention_in_Yemen_\(2015%E2%80%93present\))

### In Yemen

#### Opposition



Yemen's former president [Ali Abdullah Saleh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali_Abdullah_Saleh) was initially allied with Houthis, until they assassinated him on account of treason.

Following the call by the leader of the Houthi movement, [Abdul-Malik al-Houthi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdul-Malik_al-Houthi), tens of thousands of Yemenis of various socioeconomic backgrounds took to the streets of the rebel-controlled capital, Sanaa, to voice their anger at the Saudi intervention.[^cite_note-458]

On 21 April 2015, representatives of 19 Yemeni political parties and associations rejected UN Resolution 2216, stating that it encouraged terrorist expansion, intervened in Yemen's sovereign affairs, violated Yemen's right of self-defence and emphasized the associations' support of the Yemeni Army.[^cite_note-459][^cite_note-460]

On 23 April, a spokesman for the Houthis said UN-sponsored peace talks should continue, but only following "a complete halt of attacks" by the coalition.[^cite_note-461]

In a televised address on 24 April, Saleh called on the Houthis and other armed groups to withdraw from the territory they had seized and participate in UN-sponsored peace talks, in exchange for an end to the air campaign.[^cite_note-462] Exiled Yemeni Foreign Minister rejected the peace proposal saying that Saleh had no role in the talks.[^cite_note-463]

On 26 April, the General Authority for Archeology and Museums in Yemen condemned attacks targeting historical sites. The statement highlighted an attack that completely destroyed an ancient fortress in the [Damt District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Damt_District) of the [Ad Dali' Governorate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ad_Dali'_Governorate).[^cite_note-464] Yemeni political parties issued a letter to UN Secretary-General [Ban Ki-moon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ban_Ki-moon) requesting that he continue the peace talks. The letter emphasized that Yemen was still under attack by air, land and sea and that the existing blockade was increasing the humanitarian crisis and that education had been denied for 3 million students due to the "random attacks".[^cite_note-465]

On 2 May 2015, the Yemenis Forum of Persons With Disability stated that 300 centres and organizations had been forced to stop operations following the intervention. The organization denounced the air and sea blockade that "increased the suffering of the disabled greatly".[^cite_note-466] The same day Hussein al-Ezzi, the Houthi head of foreign relations, sent a letter addressed to Secretary General Ban seeking an end to the "unjustified Saudi aggression".[^cite_note-467] He asked the UN to seek an end to what Houthis described as blatant aggression against the country.[^cite_note-end-468]

On 7 May, 17 humanitarian agencies stressed that life-saving aid would run out in a week and emphasized the need to remove the existing blockade. The International Non-Government Organizations Forum in Yemen appealed for allowing basic materials to enter the country immediately.[^cite_note-469]

On 10 May, Houthi military spokesman Sharaf Luqman welcomed the Russian initiative, which advocated a suspension of military operations and also lifting the blockade.[^cite_note-470]

On 26 March 2017, the second anniversary of the war, over a hundred thousand Houthi supporters demonstrated in Sanaa protesting the Saudi aggression and expressing solidarity.[^cite_note-471]

#### Support



Yemen's President [Abdrabbuh Mansur Hadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdrabbuh_Mansur_Hadi) in Riyadh, Saudi Arabia, 7 May 2015

Anti-Houthi groups, especially Sunnis, while supporting the intervention did not wish for the return to power of Hadi, since they viewed him as the man "who ceded control of the capital without a fight six months ago".[^cite_note-472]

On 3 April, the Al-Islah party, the Yemeni branch of the Muslim Brotherhood, declared its support for the campaign.[^cite_note-473] Supporters of the party reportedly suffered consequences, including kidnappings and raids, as a result of this declaration.[^cite_note-474][^cite_note-475]

On 26 April, the foreign minister in Hadi's government, Riad Yaseen, rejected Saleh's calls for UN-sponsored peace talks on the ground.[^cite_note-476]

### Saudi Arabia

#### Opposition

On 5 April a firefight broke out between anti-government Shiite rioters and security forces in Saudi Arabia's [Shiite-minority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shia_Islam_in_Saudi_Arabia) in Eastern Province, with one police officer killed and three others injured.[^cite_note-477] The firefight broke out after calls in the Eastern Province to protest against the military intervention.[^cite_note-478]

On 29 April, King [Salman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salman_of_Saudi_Arabia) dismissed his appointed [crown prince](236210.md), [Muqrin of Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muqrin_of_Saudi_Arabia). Some regional political analysts speculated that the decision was precipitated by Muqrin's alleged opposition to the intervention. Salman appointed [Muhammad bin Nayef](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_bin_Nayef), who publicly announced his support of the operation, to replace Muqrin.[^cite_note-479][^cite_note-480]

#### Support

On 21 April, Saudi prince [Al-Waleed bin Talal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Waleed_bin_Talal) reportedly offered 100 [Bentleys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bentley) to participating pilots. The announcement was met with substantial criticism.[^cite_note-481]

Among the general populace, the war was popular.[^cite_note-482]

### Other coalition countries

#### Bahrain

On 3 April, Bahrainis protested against the war on Yemen.[^cite_note-483] A prominent [Bahraini opposition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bahraini_opposition) politician, Fadhel Abbas, was reportedly arrested by Bahraini authorities for condemning the bombing as "flagrant aggression".[^cite_note-484]

#### Egypt

Supporters of the [Egyptian Muslim Brotherhood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egyptian_Muslim_Brotherhood) demonstrated against Egypt's military intervention.[^cite_note-485]

#### Kuwait

Kuwaiti politician [Abdul-Hamid Dashti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdul-Hamid_Dashti) reportedly criticized the war and described it as an "act of aggression".[^cite_note-486] A prominent Kuwaiti lawyer, Khalid Al Shatti, was summoned by Kuwaiti authorities for his criticism of the Saudi government.[^cite_note-487]

On 28 April, Kuwaiti Foreign Minister [Sabah Al-Khalid Al-Sabah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sabah_Al-Khalid_Al-Sabah) stated that the only solution to the Yemen crisis was political.[^cite_note-488]

### International

Main article: [International reactions to the Saudi Arabian-led intervention in Yemen (2015–present)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_reactions_to_the_Saudi_Arabian-led_intervention_in_Yemen_\(2015%E2%80%93present\))



Foreign Ministers of the U.S., the U.K., Saudi Arabia and the United Arab Emirates, before a working dinner focused on Yemen, 19 July 2016

The [Arab League](52625.md), United States, Turkey, [OIC](47725.md) and [Hamas](13913.md) voiced support for the intervention,[^cite_note-489][^cite_note-490][^cite_note-491][^cite_note-492] but the [European Union](9317.md), Russia[^cite_note-493] and the United Nations criticised it.[^cite_note-494][^cite_note-495][^cite_note-496] The United Kingdom, and France supported the intervention,[^cite_note-497] and along with Canada have supplied the Saudi military with equipment.[^cite_note-498][^cite_note-499][^cite_note-Saudi_2016-500]

[Iran](14653.md) condemned the intervention as "US-backed aggression".[^cite_note-501] [Iran's U.N. Ambassador](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Permanent_Representative_of_Iran_to_the_United_Nations) Gholamali Khoshroo said, "those who violate international law, including international humanitarian law, should be held accountable for their acts and there should be no room for impunity."[^cite_note-502] Iraqi Prime Minister [Haidar al-Abadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haidar_al-Abadi) expressed the [Iraqi government](11752991.md)'s opposition to the intervention: "This (Yemen war) can engulf the whole region in another conflict. We don't need another [sectarian war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sectarianism) in the region."[^cite_note-503] The [Hezbollah](13919.md) secretary general criticized Saudi Arabia and its allies, saying "all invaders end up being defeated".[^cite_note-504]

The [Chinese](5405.md) foreign ministry expressed in January 2016 its support for the intervention and the Hadi government, while stressing its desire for a resumption of stability in Yemen.[^cite_note-505]

[Somalia](27358.md)'s government blamed the Saudi-led coalition for the killing of at least 42 [Somali refugees](9662371.md) off the Yemeni coast. Somali Prime Minister [Hassan Ali Khaire](53281313.md) called the attack on a boat carrying refugees "atrocious" and "appalling".[^cite_note-somali-404]



Protesters against the US-backed Saudi-led war on Yemen were led away and handcuffed by [New York](645042.md) police outside the [US mission to the UN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Mission_to_the_United_Nations) on 11 December 2017.

Asian countries, including China, India, Malaysia and Pakistan, moved within days to [evacuate their citizens from Yemen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_reactions_to_the_Saudi_Arabian-led_intervention_in_Yemen_\(2015%E2%80%93present\)#Evacuations_and_other_actions).[^cite_note-506][^cite_note-507][^cite_note-508][^cite_note-509]

On 4 April, the [ICRC](5023832.md) called for a 24-hour humanitarian ceasefire after the coalition blocked three aid shipments to Yemen.[^cite_note-reuters.com-510] Russia also called for "humanitarian pauses" in the coalition bombing campaign, bringing the idea before the [United Nations Security Council](31956.md) in a 4 April emergency meeting.[^cite_note-511] Saudi Arabia's UN ambassador raised questions over whether humanitarian pauses are the best way of delivering humanitarian assistance.[^cite_note-512] On 7 April, China renewed calls for an immediate ceasefire.[^cite_note-513]

On 10 April, the [Pakistani Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_of_Pakistan) declined a Saudi Arabian request to join the coalition. The Parliament clarified the wish to maintain a neutral diplomatic stance.[^cite_note-514]

On 16 April, a group of U.S. and UK-based Yemen scholars wrote an open letter stating that the operation was illegal under international law and calling for the UN to enforce an immediate ceasefire.[^cite_note-TWP_2015-04-18_TYS-515]

On 19 April, international aid agency [Oxfam](19698439.md) condemned Saudi Arabia over airstrikes it said hit one of its warehouses containing humanitarian supplies in Saada.[^cite_note-516]

Aid groups came out against the air campaign: [Amnesty International](18947898.md) said some of the coalition's airstrikes "appear to have failed to take necessary precautions to minimize harm to civilians and damage to civilian objects".[^cite_note-517] [Reporters without Borders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reporters_without_Borders) condemned a strike in Sanaa on 20 April that caused the deaths of four employees of Al-Yemen Al-Youm TV and injured ten others; it also condemned attacks on journalists by pro-Houthi forces.[^cite_note-518]

On 4 May, the UN called on the coalition to stop attacking Sanaa Airport to allow delivery of humanitarian aid.[^cite_note-519] On 10 May the UN Humanitarian Coordinator for Yemen stated that the attacks on Saada province were in breach of international law.[^cite_note-520] On 29 June, Secretary General Ban Ki-moon denounced a coalition airstrike that had hit a UN compound in Aden the previous day and requested a full investigation.[^cite_note-521]

[Human Rights Watch](46530.md) criticized the [UN Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Security_Council) repeatedly for "remaining almost silent on coalition abuses".[^cite_note-NYT_2015-12-22_MAY-307][^cite_note-HRW_2015-11-26_WMT-300][^cite_note-HRW_2015-12-22_SUT-522] In January 2016, an unpublished United Nations panel investigating the Saudi-led bombing campaign in Yemen uncovered "widespread and systematic" attacks on civilian targets in violation of international humanitarian law, calling UN Security Council up for an international commission of inquiry.[^cite_note-AFP_2016-01-27_UPC-523][^cite_note-theguardian-com_2016-01-27_URI-524][^cite_note-voanews-com_2016-01-28_UPA-525] Saudi Arabia had previously objected to an inquiry being set up,[^cite_note-AFP_2016-01-27_UPC-523][^cite_note-AI_2015-10-02_URY-526] and had not been supported by Western governments.[^cite_note-globalpost-com_2015-11-30_WUH-527][^cite_note-NYT_2015-12-22_SLW-528][^cite_note-news-vice-com_2016-01-07_UTH-529][^cite_note-theatlantic-com_2015-10-02_LOW-530]

In February 2016, the [Secretary-General of the UN](162415.md) [Ban Ki-moon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ban_Ki-moon) raised strong concerns over continued Saudi-led airstrikes, saying, "coalition air strikes in particular continue to strike hospitals, schools, mosques and civilian infrastructures" in Yemen. He urged States that are signatories to the [Arms Trade Treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arms_Trade_Treaty) to "control arms flows to actors that may use them in ways that breach of international humanitarian law".[^cite_note-un-org_2016-02-05_SGA-531][^cite_note-ECHO_2016-02-08_EDF-532]

In June 2016, Ban removed the Saudi-led coalition from a list of [children's rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Children's_rights) violators,[^cite_note-533] saying that Saudi Arabia threatened to cut Palestinian aid and funds to other UN programs if the coalition was not removed from the blacklist for killing children in Yemen. According to one source, there was also a threat of "clerics in Riyadh meeting to issue a [fatwa](11259.md) against the UN, declaring it anti-Muslim, which would mean no contacts of OIC members, no relations, contributions, support, to any UN projects, programs".[^cite_note-534]

In September 2016, British Foreign Secretary [Boris Johnson](19065069.md) was accused of blocking the UN inquiry into Saudi war crimes in Yemen.[^cite_note-535]

In April 2018, French President [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md) voiced support for the Saudi Arabian–led intervention in Yemen and defended France's arms sales to the Saudi-led coalition.[^cite_note-yemen-536] France authorised \$18 billion (€16 billion) in arms sales to Saudi Arabia in 2015.[^cite_note-Saudi_2016-500]

Bahri Abha – the Saudi Arabian ship arrived on 10 December 2019, at the Sagunto, Valencia port, where they were faced by Spanish Control Arms campaign organizations. Since the beginning of the Yemen war, the same ship has reportedly ferried \$162 million worth of U.S.-made arms to the kingdom. Organizations such as Amnesty International, [FundiPau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FundiPau?action=edit\&redlink=1), [Greenpeace](12233.md) and [Oxfam Intermón](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oxfam_Interm%C3%B3n) have objected to the shipment of arms from the Spanish port.[^cite_note-537]

On June 15, 2020, UN Secretary-General [António Guterres](471806.md) removed the Saudi-led coalition from a list of children's rights violators despite continued grave violations against children in Yemen.[^cite_note-538]

On 12 November 2021, in opposition to Saudi Arabia's offensive operations in the Yemen civil war, Rep. [Ilhan Omar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ilhan_Omar) introduced a joint resolution to block the sale of \$650 million US weapons to the Kingdom. The weapons sale was authorized by the Biden administration and was expected to include 280 missiles, 596 LAU-128 Missile Rail Launchers, and other equipment. Omar said in a statement, "It is simply unconscionable to sell weapons to Saudi Arabia while they continue to slaughter innocent people and starve millions in Yemen, kill and torture dissidents, and support modern-day slavery."[^cite_note-539]

On 28 December 2021, The [Special Envoy of the Secretary-General](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Envoy_of_the_Secretary-General) for Yemen, Hans Grundberg, raised an alarm about the safety of civilians in the war-torn Yemen given the escalating violence, including airstrikes carried out by the Saudi-led coalition. According to his statement, airstrikes on [Sanaa](18691665.md) resulted in loss of civilian lives, and damage to the country's infrastructure. He also underlined that violations of international humanitarian and human rights law cannot continue with impunity.[^cite_note-540]

On 29 July 2022, the United Nations [Committee Against Torture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Committee_Against_Torture) issued its findings on the United Arab Emirates after review of the States party. In the session, the committee expressed concerns regarding the country's inhuman practices despite compliance of the Convention against Torture and Other Cruel, Inhuman or Degrading Treatment or Punishment. In its finding, the committee issued concerns about allegations of torture and maltreatment against the UAE-led armed forces, related non-state armed groups, and state security agencies in the Yemen war and fight against terrorism. The committee announced a special onus on the probe and prosecution of the allegations of offenses concerning torture and ill-treatment in the said situations and demanded for a viable pathway to be introduced for the victims in order for them to seek redress, justice and rehabilitation.[^cite_note-541]

On 29 January 2021, Italy blocked arms sales to Saudi Arabia and the UAE, which ranked as biggest arms importers of Italian weapons. Then-Prime Minister [Matteo Renzi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matteo_Renzi) cited the humanitarian crisis in Yemen in which the UAE and Saudi-led coalition was involved. Renzi had previously agreed to sales of approximately 20,000 missiles worth 400 million euros.[^cite_note-542] However, Italy lifted the embargo on arms sales to the UAE, on 17 April 2023.[^cite_note-543] A month after, [Italy](14532.md) also lifted the embargo on arms sales to [Saudi Arabia](349303.md), on 31 May 2023.[^cite_note-544]

## Al-Qaeda and Islamic State

See also: [al-Qaeda insurgency in Yemen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Qaeda_insurgency_in_Yemen)

Both [al-Qaeda in the Arabian Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Qaeda_in_the_Arabian_Peninsula) (AQAP) and the [Islamic State](9087364.md) had a presence in Yemen before the Saudi-led intervention. AQAP had controlled substantial pieces of territory for some time, while the Islamic State claimed responsibility for twin bombings in Sanaa the following month which killed 140 people and injured hundreds more.[^cite_note-545]

The two groups have used the conflict to expand and consolidate, a fact accepted by the Pentagon.[^cite_note-NYT_allow_Qaeda-546] The Houthis disengaged fighting AQAP to face rival Yemeni militias at the same time as they were being hit by coalition air strikes;[^cite_note-NYT_allow_Qaeda-546][^cite_note-547] A source indicates that Yemeni troops in the south remained in their bases instead of confronting al-Qaeda militants, fearing Saudi airstrikes on any troop movements.[^cite_note-GDJ_prison_break-548] There are questions about the ability of the country to confront its Islamist militancy problem due to the major infrastructure damage caused by the war.[^cite_note-GDJ_prison_break-548]

Within weeks of the commencement of the Yemen's civil war, AQAP had exploited the chaos to [capture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Mukalla_\(2015\)) the south-eastern port city of [Mukalla](2414800.md),[^cite_note-549] along with nearby military, transport, and economic infrastructure.[^cite_note-NYT_allow_Qaeda-546] A series of prison breaks by al-Qaeda—they emptied Mukalla's jail of 300 prisoners and emptied 1,200 inmates in June 2015 from the central prison in [Taiz](545270.md)—released jailed jihadists of all ranks.[^cite_note-550][^cite_note-551] Reports indicate that Yemen's prisons had, in preceding years, reportedly become "de facto jihadi academies", as veteran militants were placed in cells alongside young, regular criminals.[^cite_note-GDJ_prison_break-548]

The coalition campaign against the Houthis in Aden in July 2015 and subsequent chaos increased AQAP and the Islamic State's presence in the city.[^cite_note-552] Residents of Aden faced a wave of bombings and shootings that prevented efforts at stabilization.[^cite_note-AFP_2016-02-09_YCK-553] AQAP conducted assassinations of judges, security officials, and police.[^cite_note-trust-org_2016-02-09_WOA-554]

On 26 August 2015, Bob Semple, a British petroleum engineer who was kidnapped and held as a hostage by Al Qaeda in Yemen, was freed by the UAE armed forces after 18 months of captivity.[^cite_note-555]

At the start of February 2016, AQAP recaptured Azzan, an important commercial city in Shabwa province.[^cite_note-556] A few weeks later, al-Qaeda fighters and Saudi-led coalition forces were seen fighting a common target; the Houthis.[^cite_note-bbc_2016-02-22-557] But the situation is different in Aden, the AQAP/ISIS and pro-Hadi that were fighting a common enemy in Taiz are enemies in Aden. On 29 February 2016, a suicide car killed 4 pro-Hadi troops in Shiek Othman district in Aden, the city that Hadi uses as a temporary capital.[^cite_note-558]

In collaboration with the United States, the United Arab Emirates has had a leading role in the fight against AQAP and the Islamic State's presence in Yemen.[^cite_note-559] In April 2016, UAE armed forces assisted Yemeni forces in retaking the city of [Mukalla](2414800.md) from AQAP during the [Battle of Mukalla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Mukalla_\(2016\)).[^cite_note-560][^cite_note-561] In August 2017, the UAE armed forces assisted a Yemeni army offensive against AQAP in [Shabwah Governorate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shabwah_Governorate).[^cite_note-562]

In an op-ed in *The Washington Post* [Yousef Al Otaiba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yousef_Al_Otaiba), the UAE ambassador to the United States, described that the intervention has reduced [AQAP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Qaeda_in_the_Arabian_Peninsula) presence in Yemen to its weakest point since 2012 with many areas previously under their control liberated.[^cite_note-WP-563] The ambassador declared that more than 2,000 militants have been removed from the battlefield, with their controlled areas now having improved security and a better delivered humanitarian and development assistance such as to the port city of [Mukalla](2414800.md) and other liberated areas.[^cite_note-WP-563] An Associated Press investigation outlined that the military coalition in Yemen actively reduced AQAP in Yemen without military intervention, instead by offering them deals and even actively recruiting them in the coalition because "they are considered as exceptional fighters".[^cite_note-564] UAE Brigadier General Musallam Al Rashidi responded to the accusations by stating that Al Qaeda cannot be reasoned with and cited that multiple of his soldiers have been killed by them.[^cite_note-565] The UAE military stated that accusations of allowing AQAP to leave with cash contradicts their primary objective of depriving AQAP of its financial strength.[^cite_note-TIDx-566] The notion of the coalition recruiting or paying AQAP has been thoroughly denied by the United States [Pentagon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pentagon) with Colonel Robert Manning, spokesperson of the Pentagon, calling the news source "patently false".[^cite_note-567] The governor of [Hadramut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hadramut) Faraj al-Bahsani, dismissed the accusations that Al Qaeda has joined with the coalition rank, explaining that if they did there would be sleeper cells and that he would be "the first one to be killed". According to *The Independent*, AQAP activity on social media as well as the number of terror attacks conducted by them has decreased since the Emirati intervention.[^cite_note-TIDx-566]

In January 2019, CNN stated that Saudi Arabia and the UAE provided al-Qaeda linked groups in Yemen with US-made military equipment including vehicles.[^cite_note-568][^cite_note-569]

On 25 June 2019, Saudi special forces announced that they captured the leader of the ISIL-YP, [Abu Osama al-Muhajer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Osama_al-Muhajer), on the 3 June along with other members including the chief financial officer of the organization.[^cite_note-570]

In April 2020, Yemeni journalist Salah Bin Laghbar revealed documents showing cooperation between Saudi-led coalition and al-Qaeda in Yemen; "An official document from the al-Humiqani tribe warns Saudi-led coalition against sending weapons to terrorist organizations through the [Al-Rashad Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Rashad_Union), [Muslim Brotherhood](20742.md) and terrorist Abdul Rahman Abu al-Harith al-Humiqani, who is affiliated with Daesh."[^cite_note-571]

## Other effects



Registration of Indian citizens evacuating from Yemen, March 2015

On 25 March 2015, [Gulf Air](377403.md), the Bahraini flag carrier airline announced the immediate suspension of service to Sanaa.[^cite_note-572] [Somali airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_airlines_of_Somalia) such as [Daallo Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daallo_Airlines) and [Jubba Airways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jubba_Airways) also encountered difficulties, as they were unable to fly over Yemen after its airspace became restricted.[^cite_note-573] On 15 April 2015, Turkish Airlines suspended all Yemen flights until 1 June.[^cite_note-574]

Following Hadi's request, the administration of the Egypt-based [Nilesat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nilesat) and Saudi-based [Arabsat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabsat), two satellite communication companies, stopped broadcasting Yemeni state-run television channels that had fallen under Houthi control. The channels included Al-Yemen, Al-Eman, [Saba News Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saba_News_Agency) and Aden TV. Armed Houthis closed down the Sanaa offices of four media outlets, including [Al Jazeera](30204052.md), Yemen Shabab and [Suhail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suhail) channels, as well as Al-Masdar's newspaper and website. Al-Saeeda channel was also stormed, but was allowed to remain open on the condition it not broadcast anti-Houthi material. Houthi Political Office member Mohammad Al-Bukhaiti said the channels were closed for supporting the coalition.[^cite_note-575]

King Salman replaced his half-brother [Muqrin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muqrin_bin_Abdulaziz) as crown prince with [Muhammad bin Nayef](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_bin_Nayef) and named his son [Mohammed bin Salman](38689358.md) as defence minister, and then-Ambassador to the United States [Adel al-Jubeir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adel_al-Jubeir) as foreign minister. Some reports linked the cabinet reshuffle to the war.[^cite_note-576][^cite_note-577] At least one political analyst suggested that Muqrin was not supportive of the military intervention, and that this cost him his position.[^cite_note-578] Prince Muqrin's Yemeni Lineage was pointed out as another possible cause.[^cite_note-579]

The exiled Yemeni government sent a request to the UN, asking for foreign troops on the ground.[^cite_note-580]

On 19 June 2015, [WikiLeaks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WikiLeaks) announced the intention of releasing over 500,000 Saudi diplomatic documents to the internet. In its statement, WikiLeaks referred to a recent electronic attack on the Saudi Foreign Ministry by a group calling itself the [Yemen Cyber Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yemen_Cyber_Army), but did not indicate whether they passed the documents to WikiLeaks.[^cite_note-581]

The [Sudanese](27421.md) [Rapid Support Forces](43053874.md) (RSF), which went on to gain international notoriety for [committing genocide](75311533.md) in [Darfur](605837.md) during the [Sudanese Civil War](73556699.md), participated in the intervention on a [mercenary](73374.md) basis. They reportedly killed civilians and destroyed infrastructure during the operation, for which they were accused of [war crimes](48626.md) by organisations such as [Human Rights Watch](46530.md).[^cite_note-582][^cite_note-583][^cite_note-584][^cite_note-585] Importantly, the RSF also traces back its [ongoing relations](79424486.md#Military_relations) with the [United Arab Emirates](69328.md) to the intervention in Yemen, which brought RSF leader [Hemedti](60669608.md) in contact with Emirati representatives.[^cite_note-586][^cite_note-587][^cite_note-588]

## Peace efforts

|  | This section needs to be **updated**. Please help update this article to reflect recent events or newly available information. *(April 2019)* |
| --- | --- |

Main article: [Yemeni peace process](34801006.md)

### Cease fire talks

On 15 May 2015, new UN envoy to Yemen [Ismail Ould Cheikh Ahmed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ismail_Ould_Cheikh_Ahmed) proposed peace talks in Geneva. Rebel spokesman Hamed al-Bokheiti said the Houthis were willing to hold talks in any "neutral" country.[^cite_note-589] Five days later the [Secretary-General of the United Nations](162415.md), [Ban Ki-moon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ban_Ki-moon) announced that peace talks would be held in Geneva starting on 28 May and urged all parties to participate.[^cite_note-590] Houthi rebels reiterated their support for the talks while exiled government officials said they would participate only if the Houthis withdrew from occupied cities.[^cite_note-591]

On 26 May, Ban announced that the peace talks were to be postponed indefinitely after exiled Yemeni officials refused to attend until rebels withdrew from all occupied cities.[^cite_note-un-org20150526BCF-592] On 6 June the UN announced that peace talks would take place on 14 June[^cite_note-593] Both the exiled officials and the Houthi group confirmed their attendance.[^cite_note-reuters.com3-361]

### 15–19 June 2015 talks

Secretary-General Ban called for a "humanitarian pause" during the Muslim holy month of [Ramadan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramadan). Peace talks between the exiled government and the Houthis concluded in Geneva without reaching a ceasefire.[^cite_note-594][^cite_note-595]

### Ramadan peace agreement

On 4 July 2015, Houthi spokesman Mohammed Abdul Salam said in a post on his Facebook page that he had met Ahmed on Friday to discuss a Ramadan truce. The US and EU announced their support for a humanitarian truce.[^cite_note-596]

On 9 July, the UN announced an unconditional truce between 10 July until the end of [Eid ul Fitr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eid_ul_Fitr) on 17 July. The Special Envoy to Yemen assured the agreement of all warring factions.[^cite_note-un-org20150709UCW-597] The truce was interrupted within an hour by airstrikes.[^cite_note-598] Coalition spokesman later added that the coalition was not bound by the truce and that any truce would be counterproductive.[^cite_note-599] It later added that it was not requested to pause by the exiled Yemeni Government.[^cite_note-600]

### Further peace talks

On 8 September 2015, [Vice News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vice_News) revealed a leaked email by UN Envoy to Yemen Ismail Ould Cheikh Ahmed. In it, the envoy confirms that Houthi rebels and the party of former president and Houthi ally Ali Abdullah Saleh have expressed willingness to accept—with some reservations—a UN Security Council resolution, approved in April. This demanded the rebels "withdraw their forces from all areas they have seized, including the capital, Sanaa". "AA/GPC agreed to a new wording on UNSC resolution 2216 that states unequivocally that they are committed to the implementation of 2216 (see document attached) with the exception of article which infringe on Yemeni sovereignty and those related to sanctions," wrote Ould Cheikh Ahmed, referring to Ansar Allah (AA)—another name for the Houthis—and Saleh's General People's Congress party (GPC). "In addition, the new text includes acceptance of the return of the current government for a period of 60 days during which a government of national unity shall be formed," wrote the envoy in the email. According to Ould Cheikh Ahmed, during talks, the Houthis gave ground on certain language, including "mandatory support by the international community for reconstruction that was in the earlier version". "The latter was particularly opposed by KSA [Kingdom of Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Saudi_Arabia) and GCC \[Gulf Cooperation Council] who did not want it to be interpreted as a form of mandatory compensation", added the UN envoy.[^cite_note-601]

On 10 September, the UN Envoy to Yemen announced that all parties had agreed to peace talks. A statement from Hadi's office following a meeting on the issue of new talks affirmed the president's "complete support for the sincere efforts exerted by the special envoy". It urged Ahmed to "exert efforts to achieve the public and honest commitment on the part of the Houthis and Saleh" to implement 14 April council resolution unconditionally.[^cite_note-602] On 13 September, the exiled Yemeni government announced that it would no longer participate in the peace talks.[^cite_note-603]

### 2016 talks

On 18 April, peace talks aimed at ending Yemen's civil war that were set to begin faltered before they could start, when delegates representing Yemen's Houthi rebels refused to attend.[^cite_note-604]

On 20 April, talks convened, based on UN Security Council resolution 2216 which called for the Houthi fighters to withdraw from areas they seized since 2014 and hand heavy weapons back to the government.[^cite_note-605]

On 6 August, the UN special envoy to Yemen, Ismail Ould Cheikh Ahmed, announced the suspension in Kuwait, where the talks were being held. He said that the negotiations were not a failure and that they would resume in a month at an undisclosed location. Mr. Ahmed is the second United Nations envoy to try to broker peace talks between the Houthis and other factions in Yemen since March 2015. His predecessor quit after similar peace talk efforts failed. After the breakdown of the talks, one of the Houthi negotiators, Nasser Bagazgooz, blamed the United Nations envoy for seeking what he said amounted to a military solution on behalf of the Saudi-led coalition.[^cite_note-606] Previous negotiations floated the idea of forming a unity government—composed of Houthi and former Hadi government leaders. But the exiled Hadi leaders have consistently rejected any deal that would diminish their power over Yemen, and the Houthis have said that they will reject any deal that does not give them a seat at the table.[^cite_note-607][^cite_note-608][^cite_note-609]

November ceasefire

The Saudi-led military coalition and Houthis arrived at a swift ceasefire agreement effective 17 November 2016, as a result of efforts of US Secretary of State John Kerry and Omani dignitaries.[^cite_note-610]

### 2020 ceasefire in response to the COVID-19 pandemic

After the United Nations urged both sides to pursue peace talks in order to respond to the [COVID-19 pandemic in Yemen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_Yemen),[^cite_note-611] Saudi-led coalition spokesman [Turki Al-Maliki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turki_Al-Maliki) announced a unilateral ceasefire beginning 9 April at noon, to support efforts to mitigate the [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md).[^cite_note-612] However, despite pledging ceasefire in [Yemen](350939.md), Saudi-led coalition carried out dozens of airstrikes in the span of a week. The Yemen Data Project stated that at least 106 Saudi-led airstrikes, across 26 raids in Yemen had been carried out by the Kingdom in just one week.[^cite_note-613] On July 2, coalition fighter jets launched scores of airstrikes on several Yemeni provinces. The operation was a response to ballistic missile and drone launchings by the Houthis against Saudi Arabia.[^cite_note-614] Both sides stepped up their attacks in September.[^cite_note-615] On 10 January 2021, U.S. Secretary of State [Mike Pompeo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Pompeo) announced plans to designate [Abdul Malik al-Houthi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdul_Malik_al-Houthi), Abd al-Khaliq Badr al-Din al-Houthi and Abdullah Yahya al Hakim as [Specially Designated Global Terrorists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Specially_Designated_Global_Terrorist).[^cite_note-616] A month later, [Antony J. Blinken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antony_J._Blinken) revoked the designation of the trio as Specially Designated Global Terrorists.[^cite_note-617] On January 2025, the [second Trump administration](78298108.md) re-designated the Houthis as a foreign terrorist organization.[^cite_note-618]
